Progress! The boot advanced past the previous crash point (53s GUEST STOP). No "GUEST STOP" in this log. The dispatch ran — `ICDSPTBL base=0x00006C00 tidx=0 P=0x00006D00 r2=0x00000000 stack=0x0000A000`.

Notice: `stack=0x0000A000` — the [KDP-4] read was 0xA000, not 0x77E0! And r2=0 — the current r2 at raise was 0x00000000. Hmm. So r1 became 0xA000 at dispatch? That's the KDP base, not the stack top... Actually the milestone shows `INTRAISE PC=0x40B24F04 r1=0x00000000` — r1=0 at raise time (the "r1=0" issue noted in summary). So [KDP-4]=0xA000. So the new r1 = 0xA000.

But more importantly: the guest is now spinning (PROGRESS every 250k) at PC=0x40B127AC/0x40B127BC/0x40B12818 with r1=0x00000000, r28 alternating 0x80BF0808/0x0000FFF8, r31=0xFFFFF510, [r31]=0x80BF0808, r24(68Kpc)=0. That's the 68K emulator spin (LR=0x40B12788). This is the same "68K-emulation compute-bound crawl" noted before? Or is it a new loop? The summary mentioned a "char-wait crawl 68K-emulation compute-bound". 

Wait — this looks like it could be the "MMU-flush"/scheduler loop? Actually PC 0x40B127xx was referenced earlier in the sched area. r1=0x00000000 though — that's worrying (r1=0 again!). But the emulator didn't crash / didn't hit PROGRESS[END]. It ran for 120s in a loop at those PCs.

Actually wait, PROGRESS lines print every 250,000 executed instructions with PC/r1/r8 etc. The pattern: PC cycles 0x40B12818 → 0x40B127AC → 0x40B127BC → 0x40B12818... LR=0x40B12788 fixed. r24(68Kpc)=0, r27(op)=0. r28 alternates 0x0000FFF8 / 0x80BF0808. WAIT. This is crucial. The codes aren't -0x725D/-0x725F — they're **0x8DA1/0x8DA2/0x8DA3**! And the logic:
- If ISR returns r8 == 0x8DA2 → continue (fall to 0x40B148C0: `bne cr0`→crash; so if r8==0x8DA2, cr0 equal → falls through)
- Then `bne cr1 → 0x40B12CB0` — if r8 != 0x8DA3 → **0x40B12CB0**. Since r8==0x8DA2, cr1 not-equal → branch taken → **0x40B12CB0 (proceed/resume)**.

So the return code chain: cr0 equal (0x8DA2) ensures not crash at cr0, then cr1 not-equal (0x8DA2 != 0x8DA3) → **jump to 0x40B12CB0** = the "resume interrupted task" path!! And cr2 equality (0x8DA1) would be the third-deep check (only reached if r8==0x8DA3).

So: **if the level-9 ISR returns 0x8DA2, the D0 handler proceeds to 0x40B12CB0 — resuming the interrupted (idle) task in place.** That's EXACTLY the "resume" behavior we want! The interrupted idle task continues, its own SCC poll consumes the queued bytes, boot proceeds.

So the cleanest fix: make the level-9 ISR *return* rather than dispatch. The ISR currently returns -0x7266/-0x7267 (negative) or dispatches (crash). If it returned **0x8DA2**, the D0 would resume the idle task.

BNE cr2 → 0x40B14660      ; r8 != 0x8DA1 → CRASH
; else r8 == 0x8DA1:
stb r8,1,[SPRG4-0x118]; b 0x40B12CB0  ; RESUME
```

0x40B148B4  cmpi cr0,r8,0x8DA2

1. The dispatch tables DID work mechanically: rfi reached 0x40B26880 with the tables correctly populated.

# EFI-Mac-Emulator build
#
# Cross-builds a UEFI x86_64 application (EFI-Mac-Emulator.efi) using a
# clang/LLVM toolchain targeting PE/COFF, linked with lld-link. GNU-EFI
# provides the UEFI headers and a small runtime library.
#
# Requirements:
#   macOS:  brew install llvm lld  (llvm-objdump comes with llvm)
#   Windows: chocolatey install llvm (or add LLVM\bin to PATH), GNU make,
#            git-bash on PATH so `make` finds /bin/sh
#   Linux:  apt install clang lld
#
# Host detection: on macOS the Homebrew prefix is used if available; other
# hosts resolve clang/lld-link from PATH. Override via
# make CC=/path/to/clang LLD=/path/to/lld-link

SHELL   := /bin/sh

# --- Toolchain discovery (override via make CC=/path/to/clang LLD=/path/to/lld-link) ---
UNAME_S := $(shell uname -s)
ifeq ($(UNAME_S),Darwin)
LLVM_PREFIX := $(shell brew --prefix llvm 2>/dev/null || echo /opt/homebrew/opt/llvm)
LLD_PREFIX  := $(shell brew --prefix lld  2>/dev/null || echo /opt/homebrew/opt/lld)
CC      = $(LLVM_PREFIX)/bin/clang
LLD     ?= $(LLD_PREFIX)/bin/lld-link
OBJDUMP = $(LLVM_PREFIX)/bin/llvm-objdump
else
# Plain "?=" would let a host environment CC (e.g. git-bash's CC=cc) leak in;
# use ":=" so the PATH-resolved clang wins unless overridden on the command line.
CC      := clang
LLD     := lld-link
OBJDUMP := llvm-objdump
endif

# --- Layout ---
GNUEFI_DIR  := third_party/gnu-efi
GNUEFI_URL  := https://git.code.sf.net/p/gnu-efi/code
BUILD_DIR   := build
OBJ_GNUEFI  := $(BUILD_DIR)/gnuefi
OBJ_SRC     := $(BUILD_DIR)/src

```

**Output:**

# Boot the built EFI-Mac-Emulator.efi under QEMU + OVMF on Windows.
# Usage (PowerShell):
#   .\scripts\run-qemu-windows.ps1                      # no Mac disc attached
#   .\scripts\run-qemu-windows.ps1 -MacDisc mac_discs\System7_5_3.img
#   .\scripts\run-qemu-windows.ps1 -MacDisc mac_discs\System7_5_3.img `
#       -OldWorldRom "mac_roms\1997-11 - 79D68D63 - Power Mac G3 desktop.ROM"
#   .\scripts\run-qemu-windows.ps1 -MacDisc mac_discs\MacOS 8 (...8.1...).iso
#
# Validation matrix targets (see TODO.md "Validation Matrix"):
#   Mac OS 9.2.2  -MacDisc mac_discs\Apple Mac OS 9.2.2 [PowerMac G4].7z  (New World)
#   Mac OS 8.1    -MacDisc mac_discs\MacOS 8 (...8.1...).iso             (New World)
#   System 7.5.3  -MacDisc mac_discs\System7_5_3.img  + -OldWorldRom      (Old World)
#   Old World ROM -OldWorldRom mac_roms\...Power Mac 7100 (newer).ROM     (Old World)
# To attach an Old World ROM dump, pass -OldWorldRom; it is staged onto the ESP
# at \System\MacOS\ROM (the bootlayer's first-priority ROM source for Old World).
#
# Prereqs: chocolatey llvm + qemu; OVMF_CODE_4M.fd / OVMF_VARS_4M.fd unpacked
# from the Debian ovmf package into $env:TEMP\opencode\ovmf (see BUILD_INSTRUCTIONS.md).
param(
    [string]$Efi   = "$PSScriptRoot\..\build\EFI-Mac-Emulator.efi",
    [string]$Esp   = "$env:TEMP\opencode\esp",
    [string]$Ovmf  = "$env:TEMP\opencode\ovmf",
    [string]$MacDisc = "",
    [string]$OldWorldRom = "",
    [switch]$NoReboot,
    [int]$Seconds  = 25,

    # ---- Early exit -------------------------------------------------------
    # A boot soak is a ceiling, not a target: once the guest has crashed, or has
    # stopped emitting serial output, the remaining window yields nothing and
    # costs real wall-clock time on every iteration. These switches end the run
    # as soon as the outcome is already determined. -Seconds stays the ceiling.
    [switch]$NoStopOnMarker,          # disable all early-exit detection
    [int]$StallSeconds = 60,          # stop after this long with no serial output
    [string[]]$StopOnPattern = @(),   # extra progress markers; stop when one appears
    [string[]]$TimelinePattern = @(), # report first-seen wall-clock time for each
    [string[]]$FatalMarkers = @(
        "GUEST STOP",                 # guest hit a reserved/unsupported opcode
        "budget stop"                 # emulator's own instruction-budget stop
    )

```

## Objective
- Deliver SCC EXT wake through the guest's D0 level-9 dispatch so the 4 queued 'g' CR 'g' CR SCC bytes drain and the deterministic EFIMac boot advances past the second-NK-init idle sell (toward the warm/cold divergence at 0x40B126E8).
- The dispatch must resume the preempted task's continuation (sell-glue Next = 0x40B24F08) with a correct MSR/EE — NOT the SCC pump 0x40B26880, which re-acquires a held spinlock and deadlocks.

## Important Details
(keep from prior + update)
- User decision: no synthetic interrupts; keep AUTORESUME 'g' CR 'g' CR feeder; FIFO 64B, at raise head=16 tail=12; raise at PC=0x40B24F04 r1=0 MSR=0x9002.
- Dispatch target decision (change): P[0] = Next (0x40B24F08 = sell-glue continuation of the preempted idle give-up). Resumed GPRs are still live in the vCPU (PPC exceptions don't auto-save); dispatch only rewrites r1 (stack table) and r2. Give-up re-runs sc 0x2E and the loop recycles; proved stable (no crash/deadlock).
- SCC pump 0x40B26880 deadlocks: takes spinlock [r1+0xF510] ([KDP+0xF510]=0x19F10) whose value 0x80BF0808 is held by the interrupted context; spin at 0x40B127AC/0x40B12818; never released. Reader 0x40B272E0 locks [r1+0xF4D0] similarly — avoid both as dispatch targets.
- SRR1 source (real): ISR 0x40B148E0 tail — 0x40B14994 lwz r17,0x648(r1) (→mtlr @0x40B149B8); 0x40B1499C lwz r19,0xF69C(r1) — displacement SIGNED = r1-0x964; rlwinm mask keeps bits 0-15 & 18-31; 0x40B149BC mtspr SRR0,r18; 0x40B149C0 mtspr SRR1,r19; 0x40B149C4 lwzx r1,r9,r16 — stack-table switch happens AFTER the SRR1 read. 0x40B14A04 = rfi.
- Seed attempts: edit1 wrote [Stack-0x964] = [0x969C]; edit2 wrote [Stack+0xF69C] — SAME address (0xF69C signed = -0x964). Both ineffective: ICDCHECK shows [0x969C]=0xD032 at dispatch (guest D0 flow rewrites it; 0xD032 is a sane MSR, same family as RETTASK's 0x2D032), yet SRR1 = 0x4BF620C4 ≠ mask(0xD032)=0xD032 ⇒ r1 at 0x40B1499C is NOT 0xA000 (unresolved).
- ICDCHECK (60s run): r1now=0xA000, SPRG0=Spr[272]=0xA000, SPRG4=Spr[276]=0, [0x969C]=0xD032. SPRG numbering: ppcd labels vs emulator Spr[] indices mismatch — KCALLSAVE's "mfspr r1,SPRG4" must read 0xA000 or the flow would fault; emulator apparently serves the guest's SPRG4 read from Spr[272].
- KCALLSAVE 0x40B13D40: mfspr r1,SPRG4; saves r0/r7-r13 to ecb[r6+0x104..0x16C] (r6=[KDP-0x14], ECB 0x9CE8); mfspr r10,SRR0; mfspr r11,SRR1; mfcr r13; mfspr r12,274; or r8,r1,r1; lwz r7,[r1-0x10]; lwz r1,[r1-4] (switch to KDP stack); blr. 0x40B238AC = saves r14-r31 to ecb[r6+0x174..0x1FC], no r1 change. So r1 path: [SPRG4-4] → ISR; but observed contradicting → probe needed.
- D0 0x40B14880: bl KCALLSAVE; rlwinm r9,r11,0,0x10,0x10; beq→0x40B13AB0; lwz r9,[r8+0xFCC8] (signed = r8-0x338 = [0x9CC8] IC); lwz r9,[r9+0x20] (IntEvents1); cmpi≥2 else 0x40B14660 crash; bl 0x40B238AC; li r9,9; stw [r8-0x238]; li r8,1; bl ISR; bl 0x40B2391C (FP/R restore — NOT a device servicer; returns low r8, so return-code resume paths are dead); cmpi 0x8DA2→crash; 0x8DA3→0x40B12CB0; 0x8DA1→stb [KDP-0x118]=1→0x40B12CB0. Dispatcher rfi is the only viable route.
- Guard state: InSellPhase defers DEC at loop bottom; EXT allowed through sell; [KDP-0x338]=[0x9CC8]=ICCB 0x81C0; mirrors +0x20=3, +0x38=2, +0x44=0x3FFFFFFF, +0x4C=0x75C0; tables at 0x6C00 (A at 0x6C00, P at 0x6D00, B at 0x6D20; A[0x24]=0x6D00; [IC+0x3C]=0x6C00, [IC+0x40]=0x6D20).

- **Dispatch target decision (changed):** P[0] = `Next` = 0x40B24F08 (sell-glue continuation of the preempted idle give-up). Resumed GPRs are still live in the vCPU (PPC exceptions don't auto-save); dispatch only rewrites r1/r2. The resumed give-up re-runs `sc 0x2E` and the loop recycles — empirically stable (no crash, no deadlock), but boot does **not** advance.
- **SCC pump 0x40B26880 deadlocks** (why it was abandoned): it re-acquires spinlock `[r1+0xF510]` = `[KDP+0xF510]` = `[0x19F10]`, held by the interrupted context (value 0x80BF0808); spins at 0x40B127AC/0x40B12818 with no releaser. Reader 0x40B272E0 takes `[r1+0xF4D0]` similarly — avoid both as dispatch targets.
- **SRR1 source (real, verified from ROM):** ISR 0x40B148E0 tail — `0x40B14994 lwz r17,0x648(r1)` (→ `mtspr LR` @0x40B149B8); `0x40B1499C lwz r19,0xF69C(r1)` — displacement is **signed = r1 − 0x964**; `rlwinm` keeps bits 0-15 & 18-31; `0x40B149BC mtspr SRR0,r18`; `0x40B149C0 mtspr SRR1,r19`; `0x40B149C4 lwzx r1,r9,r16` — **the stack-table switch happens AFTER the SRR1 read**; `0x40B14A04` = rfi.

Hmm actually — the 0xC00 VECDISP: `VecTbl=0xA360 offset=0x30 handler=0x40B14AC0` — the 0xC00 vector handler is the ROM's sc handler, and the SCSEVRET r3=0 comes with LR=0xBDFC — meaning the ROM handler itself branched to the fake installed code finally. The fake code returned r3=0. So overwriting 0xBDFC with the faithful version works.

lwz r4, 0x9CC8   (r4 = ICCB base? actually ICDK call: events at [r8-0x338]... simpler: read KDP events directly)
```

Actually the FKOV: events source = interrupt controller base = IC = 0x81C0; events at [IC+0x20]? we keep +0x20=3. Let me read: r4=0x81C0 → [r4+0x20]=3. So: `li r3,0; lis r4,0x0000? no — 0x81C0: lis r4,0x0; ori r4,r4,0x81C0; lwz r5,0x20(r4); cmpwi r5,0; beq done(r3=0); li r3,1; rfi`

Even simpler — I'll write it in the emulator at seed time (already doing that at lines 6740-6741). Let me modify that seed.

Then the guest's wake path (0x40B24FDC r3≠0 branch → twi → r31=0x989680 loop → ... b 0x40B24F48 → idles with banner)... hmm wait, wake path re-enters the SAME idle via 0x40B25000 → 0x40B24F48. That seems like it doesn't do boot work either.

Hmm, but hold on: 0x40B25028: `7E088378` mr r8,r16; `7E298B78` mr r9,r17; `3BE2FCC0` addis r31,r2,0xFCC0??; ... `4BFED6DD` bl 0x40B12704?? = "4BFED6DE" target: LI=-0x12922? Compute: bl at 0x40B25024: 0x4BFED6DD → LI = 0xFED6DE (sign-extend 24 bits: 0xFED6DE & 0x800000 → negative: 0xFED6DE - 0x1000000 = -0x12922) → target = 0x40B25024 - 0x12922 + ... let me do properly: target = 0x40B25024 + (sign-extend(0xFED6DE<<2)). 0xFED6DE<<1? PowerPC branch offset = (LI << 2) sign-extended from 24 bits. LI=0xFED6DE (24 bits) → sign-flag at bit23=1 → negative value = 0xFED6DE - 0x1000000 = -0x12922 (=-0x2921*4? no -0x12922 = -(0x12922)). (0xFED6DE<<2) sign-ext... simpler: -0x12922*4 = -0x4A488? Hmm: value = casts to int: 0xFED6DE = 16693982? no signed = 16693982-16777216 = -83234. <<2 = -332936 = -0x51488. target = 0x40B25024 - 0x51488 = 0x40AD3B9C?? That's outside 0x40B1xxxx. Hmm that doesn't look right. Let me recompute: 0x40B25024 = 0x40B25024. -0x51488 = 0x40AD3B9C. So bl to 0x40AD3B9C. Unclear. Probably the wake path goes to some other routine then eventually loops. 

OK the wake path for the idle task seems to eventually return to idle. So forcibly waking the idle task won't advance boot. The idle task wakes and re-idles — the ACTUAL boot work is in ANOTHER task. 

So the fundamental problem: wake the RIGHT task with the RIGHT PC. On real HW, after the console task reads 'g'CR'g'CR it signals the "SHIELD"/"boot continuation" task. The AUTORESUME records (0x40B2751C, 0x40B26548) are the console task's PCs. Those tasks already RAN and consumed the bytes; boot still stalled at IDLE. Which means the continuation needs one more EVENT to proceed (e.g., a timer interrupt / DEC firing / another EXT scalar).

## Summary — SCC-EXT dispatch effort

**Goal:** Deliver a fabricated SCC EXT wake through the guest's D0 level-9 dispatch so the queued `'g' CR 'g' CR` SCC bytes advance the deterministic EFIMac boot past the second-NK-init idle sell.

**What we proved works (mechanism is solid):**
- The fabricated EXT dispatch is now a clean, repeatable non-preempting task switch into the preempted task's sell-glue continuation (`P[0] = Next` = 0x40B24F08). Hundreds of dispatches across soaks (300 s / 180 s / 60 s) with **zero** spinlock, crash, or GUEST STOP.
- The guest's raw SRR1 slot at the ISR (`lwz r19,0xF69C(r1)`, 0x40B1499C) is genuinely **not** `[0x969C]` (that slot holds 0xD032; the load yields 0x1032/0x4BF620C4) — so the correct fix is forcing SRR1 at the dispatch rfi, which is now implemented and verified (resumed MSR = 0x9002, EE on).
- D0 internals mapped: KCALLSAVE (0x40B13D40), ISR tail, 0x40B238AC, 0x40B2391C = FP restore only, dispatch rfi 0x40B14A04, D0 return-code paths (0x8DA1/0x8DA3) unreachable/dead. SCC pump 0x40B26880 deadlocks on a held spinlock (`[r1+0xF510]` = 0x80BF0808); reader 0x40B272E0 similarly.

**Why boot still stalls (the wall):**
- Resuming the idle task's give-up is a structural dead end: that code (`0x40B24F04+`) is the **idle task's NK-panic banner** ("idle task René Alan Jim Alex Derrick…"); its wake path is a 1 s delay → NKPANIC (`NKPANIC@0x40B272EC`, `[01]..[300]`).
- Our installed give-up stub (`li r3,0; rfi` at 0xBDFC) always returns r3=0 → the guest times out and re-sells forever (2760 VECDISP 0xC00 + 2725 SCSEVRET r3=0; DEC decrements but never fires).
- The 4 bytes were consumed **before** the raise (AUTORESUME×4, SCCPOLL×3) and boot still stalled — the missing wake isn't more SCC input; it's some other event (DEC/1 s timeout, warm CSR flag at 0x40B126E8, or an unemulated KDP service).

**Working tree state:** heavily instrumented `interpreter.c` (+~3k lines of probes/feeds: AUTORESUME feeder, KDP338/ICBLK/ICMIRROR/ICDSPTBL/ICDCHECK, forced SRR1 at dispatch rfi, InSellPhase DEC guard, give-up stub seeding). `bootloader_impl.c` kept clean reverts. Docs (`FINAL_SUMMARY.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, etc.) reflect an earlier progress state and are ahead/behind inconsistently. Nothing committed.

**Build note:** use `bash -c 'export PATH="/mnt/c/Program Files/LLVM/bin:$PATH"; make CC=clang.exe LLD=lld-link.exe OBJDUMP=llvm-objdump.exe -j8'` (WSL bash can't find bare `clang`); soap via `scripts/run-qemu-windows.ps1 -NoReboot -Seconds 180 -StallSeconds 25`.

**When you want to resume:** the three candidate directions are still open — (1) trace/identify the exact wake event the "Resuming" tail blocks on, (2) pivot the dispatch target to the console task's real SCC dequeue (0x40B26548/0x40B263E0) with restored context, or (3) emulate real give-up wake semantics in the 0x2E stub. Next step at that point: probe the give-up's wake-event mask and the 0x40B126E8 warm/CSR branch to pick the right one.

-## Project Overview
-
-A UEFI-based emulator for running classic Mac OS (System 7, Mac OS 8, and
-Mac OS 9) on modern Intel x86_64 computers.
+**Terminology:** EFIMac is a **heavy UEFI bootloader** for classic Mac OS on
+x86_64, not an emulator. It stages a PowerPC Mac boot environment from UEFI
+standard protocols and then executes the installed firmware (and the OS it
+boots) for real on a PowerPC interpreter.
 
 ## What Exists Today
 
-### Research & Analysis (Phase 1) — Complete
-- Analyzed existing Mac emulators (SheepShaver, Basilisk II, QEMU, DingusPPC)
-- Studied PowerPC vs 68k architecture differences
-- Documented UEFI specifications and implementation guidelines
+- **PowerPC interpreter with a continuous fetch-execute loop**
+  (`src/cpu/interpreter.c`): fixed 32-bit big-endian opcodes, GPR/SPR/FPU
+  state, multi-region guest memory map with read-only ROM enforcement, a
+  timebase/decrementer scheduler tick, and exception delivery through the
+  firmware's own `VecTbl` (SPRG3). Includes binary instrumentation for boot
+  chasing (milestone logs, capped probes, a 4096-entry PC/register tail ring).
+- **UEFI hardware layer** (`src/platform`, `src/hardware`): GOP framebuffer,
+  Block I/O / Simple File System, Simple Network Protocol, audio ring buffer;
+  an HFS/HFS+ reader (`src/fs/hfs.c`) reads classic discs in place.
+- **Firmware sourcing** (`src/boot`): priority-ordered ROM install — ESP Old
+  World ROM path, ESP `Mac OS ROM`, HFS-discovered New World ROM (verified from
+  a real Mac OS 9.2.2 install disc), demo ROM fallback.
+- **System Folder / driver staging**: System, Finder, and up to 64 Extensions
+  staged with 0 failures on System 7.5.3 / Mac OS 8.1 / Mac OS 9.2.2 discs.
 
-### Design & Scaffolding (Phase 2, partial)
-- Module interface headers for all six subsystems:
-  - `src/cpu/translation.h` — CPU translation layer
-  - `src/memory/manager.h` — memory manager

-## What Is NOT Implemented
+With a Mac OS 9.2.2 install disc, the `Mac OS ROM` (2,763,530 bytes) is
+discovered, installed read-only, and executed continuously:
 
-- **CPU translation is not real.** `PpcTranslateInstruction` decodes a small
-  number of PowerPC opcodes but does not actually translate or execute
-  anything. There is no interpreter and no dynamic recompilation.
-- **Memory management is not implemented.** The memory manager initializes a
-  context and reads the UEFI memory map but performs no guest memory emulation.
-- **No device emulation.** Graphics/audio/storage/network functions allocate or
-  print status but do not emulate hardware.
-- **No working bootloader.** Kernel loading is simulated with hard-coded values;
-  nothing loads a real Mac OS ROM or kernel image.
-- **Emulation does not run yet.** The project builds a valid x86_64 UEFI
-  application (`build/EFI-Mac-Emulator.efi`) via GNU-EFI + clang/lld-link, but
-  the application only initializes scaffolding and prints status.

+- Task creation with PPC/68K interleave (`RETTASK`, `DRYIELD` round-trips).

-## PowerPC Interpreter
+## PowerPC Interpreter and Guest Execution
 
 `src/cpu/interpreter.c` decodes and executes fixed 32-bit big-endian PowerPC
 opcodes with a register file (32 GPRs, CR, CTR, LR, MSR, SRR0/1, FP registers +
 FPSCR), big-endian guest memory access, FPU core (opcodes 48-63, gated on
-MSR[FP] with the FP-unavailable exception at `0x800`), and exception dispatch
-(program `0x700`, FP `0x800`). Execution today is block-at-a-time
-(`PpcExecuteBlock`): small hand-checked programs run from guest RAM and the
-demo ROM's reset vector. There is no MMU, no timer/interrupt injection, and no
-continuous fetch-execute loop — the ROM window is never executed for real.
+MSR[FP] with the FP-unavailable exception), and a continuous fetch-execute loop.
+
+Implemented guest-facing behavior:
+
+- **Timebase/decrementer tick.** Each instruction advances `TBL/TBU` and `DEC`
+  by `PPC_TIMEBASE_SCALE`; a negative DEC with `MSR.EE` set and the NK's
+  `mtspr`-armed decrementer raises the decrementer exception. The NK scheduler
+  run-loop, park/wake, and `g_BootDecGate` deferral policy are handled in this
+  loop so the boot-tail handoff is never preempted before the stack is
+  established.
+- **Exception delivery through the firmware's vector table.** The firmware's
+  `VecTbl` base is read from SPRG3; entries for vectors `0x100..0xF00` are
+  dispatched as the firmware's own firmware-vector stubs would, including the
+  interrupted `r1`/`r7`/LR deposit into `[KDP-0x4]` / `[KDP-0x10]` / SPRG1/2 the
+  NK's `InterruptSave` expects. Installed vectors seen in real boots:
+  `0x500 external 0x40B14880`, `0x700 program/KCall 0x40B14700`, `0x900
+  decrementer 0x40B13200`, `0xC00 syscall 0x40B14AC0`, etc.
+- **Device windows.** Loads/stores outside guest regions decode as device
+  accesses; the SCC at `0x20000` is one (see above). Unmapped reads return 0.
+- **Instrumentation.** The interpreter carries a debug logging path
